# OpenGrid Orchestrator — Non-Functional Test Cases (Performance, Reliability, Chaos, Disaster Recovery, Security, Operability)

Status: v0.1 · 2026-09-25 · Author: independent performance, reliability and security test engineer · Audience:
implementers, SRE, security analysts, adversarial reviewers, judges and the user · Companions:
[`01-test-strategy.md`](01-test-strategy.md) (normative for levels, environments E1–E6, gates G0–G5, priorities and the
`Covers:` grammar), [`02-test-cases-functional.md`](02-test-cases-functional.md) (`TC-FUN`, `TC-INT`, `TC-UI`, `TC-UX`,
`TC-E2E`) and `04-traceability-matrix.md` (generated from the `Covers:` field of every test case).

Read `../00-brief.md` and `../00-decision-register.md` first. This document uses their vocabulary, customer-type codes, service
map and binding decisions (D0a–D5, R1–R16) without repeating them. **Documentation only:** nothing here has been built,
installed, executed or scanned. Every pass criterion is either taken from the document that owns the number (cited by its
ID), taken from the interim values of `01-test-strategy.md` §13 (cited as C-NN), or labelled **(A)** — an assumption
proposed here, with the reason — so that a failed test can always be traced to a requirement or to an assumption that
needs a decision. Reviewer-sourced numbers keep the label "reviewer proposal — unverified" (R11).

---

## How to read this document

Every test case is one row of a table with ten fields: **ID**, **title**, **objective**, **setup / environment**, **load or
attack profile**, **steps**, **measurable pass criteria**, **priority** (`P1·MVP`, `P2·MVP`, `P2` or `P3`, §1.4),
**automation** (`auto` or `manual`, with its cadence) and **Covers** (the requirement, target, failure-mode, threat, control
and decision IDs the case proves). Sections 2–7 hold the cases; section 8 summarises counts, the MVP subset and the
calendar; section 9 holds the coverage tables; sections 10–11 close with open questions, assumptions and cross-references.

| Section | Content | Test IDs |
|---|---|---|
| §1 | Scope, conventions, environments, load profiles, measurement rules, safety rails, parameter set | — |
| §2 | Performance and scale | `TC-PERF-001…067`, `TC-PERF-701…705` |
| §3 | Reliability and resilience | `TC-NFR-001…043` |
| §4 | Compound chaos scenarios (new) and the execution index of the existing chaos catalogue | `TC-CHAOS-600…627` (new); `TC-CHAOS-001…015`, `101…538` referenced |
| §5 | Disaster recovery | `TC-DR-001…020` |
| §6 | Security, including the red-team exercise plan, market conduct, personnel security and the security conditions per customer type | `TC-SEC-001…154`, `TC-SEC-701…715` |
| §7 | Operability and platform | `TC-NFR-044…080` |
| §8 | Counts by type and priority, MVP subset, G4 and decommissioning set, execution calendar, entry and exit criteria | — |
| §9 | Coverage tables (architecture NFRs, platform NFRs, PT, SLO, ST, product NFRs, binding decisions, top threat-model risks) | — |
| §10 | Open questions and assumptions | — |
| §11 | Cross-references | — |

### Which judging criteria (brief §2) this document serves

| Criterion | How |
|---|---|
| Performance (10) | §2 turns every latency, throughput, memory and solve-time claim into a measured pass criterion at 10,000 hubs on the real node and at 100,000 synthetic hubs in a production-like cluster — "measured, not asserted" |
| Completeness (15) | §3–§5 prove the end-to-end path (data in → decision → dispatch → verified delivery → settlement) survives dependency loss, node loss, compound faults and restore |
| Technical depth (15) | Fencing epochs, fail-static leases, Merkle-anchored audit verification after restore, property-based guardian tests, protocol fuzzing, red-team scenarios per attack tree |
| The problem (15) | Zero-tolerance invariants (homeowner reserve, double allocation, rebound, grid-stress envelopes) are checked in every run, including under attack |
| The "why" (15) | Tests prove firm-first allocation and the guardian's independence hold when parts fail or are attacked, which is what makes "one fleet, many buyers" safe to sell |
| Usability (10) | Alert, runbook and upgrade drills (§7) prove an operator can run the system tomorrow; the migration rehearsal (§5) proves it can move |
| Insight quality (10) | `AT_RISK` lead time, time-to-explain an invoice line and per-row MTTD/MTTR are measured outputs |
| Creativity (10) | Compound chaos scenarios that cross protocol, market, security and AI boundaries (§4) |

---

## 1. Scope, conventions and test environments

### 1.1 What this document owns and what it references

| Item | Owner | This document |
|---|---|---|
| `TC-PERF-*`, `TC-NFR-*`, `TC-SEC-*`, `TC-DR-*` bodies | **this document** | normative |
| `TC-SEC-701…715`, `TC-PERF-701…705` (placeholders allocated by `../02-architecture/07-scada-integration.md` §11.6) | **this document** (bodies) | written here with the IDs and pass criteria 07 allocated |
| New compound chaos scenarios `TC-CHAOS-600…627` | **this document** | normative |
| Single-fault chaos tests `TC-CHAOS-101…538` and game days `TC-CHAOS-001…015` | `../02-architecture/05-failure-modes-and-recovery.md` (one per FM row: injector, fault, rate, judged by its §6.5) | **referenced, never restated**; §4.1 schedules them and binds them to the environments and load profiles defined here |
| `TC-CHAOS-282…299` (FM-SCADA-022…039, proposed by 07 §9 for import into 05) | 07 → 05 | referenced as for the 05 catalogue |
| `TC-FUN-*`, `TC-INT-*` (including 07's `TC-INT-701…742`), `TC-E2E-*`, `TC-UI-*`, `TC-UX-*` | `02-test-cases-functional.md` | referenced only where a non-functional case depends on a functional precondition |
| Test levels, harness (`agent-sim` HC-AS-*, `grid-sim` HC-GS-*, shared tooling HC-TL-*), fixtures (FX-*), environments E1–E6, severity, waivers, gates G0–G5 | `01-test-strategy.md` | applied as written; §8.5 lists the non-functional entry and exit criteria per gate |

### 1.2 Test-case record

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| ID | `TC-<TYPE>-NNN`, TYPE ∈ {PERF, NFR, SEC, CHAOS, DR} (brief §7) |
| Title | Short name |
| Objective | The property proven and why it matters |
| Setup / environment | Environment code E1–E6 (§1.5), deployment profile, data set, preconditions |
| Load or attack profile | Load-profile code (§1.6), fault injections, attack traffic; for chaos cases also **blast radius** and **abort** conditions |
| Steps | Numbered, executable steps |
| Pass criteria | Numbered, measurable criteria; every threshold cites its source ID, a strategy interim value (C-NN) or is labelled (A) |
| Priority | `P1·MVP`, `P2·MVP`, `P2` or `P3` (§1.4) — the strategy's notation |
| Automation | `auto` or `manual` (strategy §0.3), followed by `·` and the cadence (§1.4) |
| Covers | `Covers: ID, ID, …` — comma-separated IDs, no ranges (§1.3) |

### 1.3 The `Covers:` field and ID namespaces

The `Covers:` field follows the grammar and rules of `01-test-strategy.md` §7.2 (comma-and-space separated, one ID per
item, no ranges, wildcards or prose; an ID is listed only when the case's pass criteria verify its acceptance criterion or a
stated part of it). Rule 4 of §7.2 assigns the non-functional namespaces to this document, so the grammar's `id` production
is **extended here** with the productions below (a change request to §7.2 is raised in §10 Q18). Every ID used in this
document was checked against its owning document; the owners are:

| ID pattern in `Covers:` | Owning document | In strategy §7.2 grammar? |
|---|---|---|
| `NFR-001…034` | `../02-architecture/01-system-architecture.md` §1 (architecture-level NFRs; see the collision rule below) | extension |
| `NFR-500…552`, `PT-01…12`, `SLO-01…12`, `DASH-501…520`, `ALR-500…559`, `RB-500…527` | `../02-architecture/06-platform-and-operations.md` | extension |
| `FM-*`, `ALR-001…299`, `RB-001…068` | `../02-architecture/05-failure-modes-and-recovery.md` | `FM` yes; `ALR`, `RB` extension |
| `FM-SCADA-022…052`, `ALR-260…269` (proposed for import into 05), `FR-SCADA-*` | `../02-architecture/07-scada-integration.md` | `FM`, `FR` yes; `ALR` extension |
| `FR-DE-*` | `../02-architecture/03-decision-engine.md` | yes |
| `TH-*`, `AT-A…AT-L`, `AB-*`, `SC-*`, `RR-*` | `../03-security/01-threat-model.md` | `TH` yes; others extension |
| `CTL-*`, `FR-SEC-101…203`, `DET-*`, `IRP-*`, `ST-*` | `../03-security/02-security-architecture.md` | `CTL`, `FR` yes; others extension |
| `FR-SEC-001…015`, `FR-OPS-*`, `FR-SCAD-*`, `FR-SAFE-*`, `FR-DISP-*`, `FR-TWIN-*`, `FR-CTR-*`, `FR-INT-*` | `../01-product/02-functional-requirements.md` | yes |
| `KPI-*` | `../01-product/01-vision-scope-personas.md` | yes |
| `UI-*` | `../04-ui/01-ui-ux-specification.md` | yes |
| `D0b`, `D1`, `D2`, `D4`, `D5`, `R1…R16` | `../00-decision-register.md` | yes |

Extension productions (ABNF, same style as §7.2):

```text
nfr   = "NFR-" 3DIGIT                  ; 001-034 architecture, 500-552 platform
pt    = "PT-" 2DIGIT
slo   = "SLO-" 2DIGIT
st    = "ST-" 2DIGIT
alr   = "ALR-" 3DIGIT
rb    = "RB-" 3DIGIT
dash  = "DASH-" 3DIGIT
det   = "DET-" 3DIGIT
irp   = "IRP-" 2DIGIT
at    = "AT-" ALPHA
ab    = "AB-" 2DIGIT
sc    = "SC-" 2DIGIT
rr    = "RR-" 2DIGIT
```

**Decision IDs.** Following strategy §7.2 rule 5, a register decision or resolution is listed when the verdict would change
if the decision changed — for example every case whose thresholds come from R3 lists `R3`, and the two cases whose verdict
depends on the open R16 list `R16`. Brief D4 has three parts — (a) command order, (b) confirmation of critical-impact
commands, (c) secure SCADA communication; `Covers:` uses the register ID `D4` and prose names the part as D4(a), D4(b) or
D4(c). §9.7 maps every decision to its cases.

**ID collision (flagged, §10 Q1; strategy C-11 and Q-T3).** `../01-product/02-functional-requirements.md` §4 also numbers its
32 product-level non-functional requirements `NFR-001…032`, with meanings that differ from the architecture's `NFR-001…034`
(for example, NFR-212 is hypertable performance, architecture NFR-012 is time to full output). To keep this document
unambiguous, a bare `NFR-0NN` in a `Covers:` field **always means the architecture NFR**; product-level NFRs are traced
through the mapping table in §9.6. The current generator (`build_traceability.py`) counts a bare `NFR-0NN` for both
documents, so its product-NFR column is not meaningful until one set is renumbered.

**Generator hygiene.** The generator treats any line that contains both `Covers:` and a test ID as a test case; prose in
this document therefore never puts the two on one line.

### 1.4 Priority, MVP and automation codes

Priorities use the strategy's notation and semantics (`01-test-strategy.md` §0.3); the non-functional reading of each is
stated so that every assignment in §2–§7 can be challenged.

| Priority | Strategy meaning | Applied to non-functional cases |
|---|---|---|
| **`P1·MVP`** | Safety, a zero-tolerance invariant, a binding decision (D1–D5) or the core chain; blocks the judged demo (G3) and every later gate; every P1 is in the MVP set; never waivable (strategy §8.4) | The case proves the control path stays safe under load, failure or attack: kill switch and stop ramps (D2, R4), command ordering, replay and approvals (D4, R3), guardian as sole signer and fencing (R1, R8), homeowner reserve and grid-stress envelopes under faults, no personal data leaving the platform (D5), no OOM of the control path inside 10,496 MiB (R2), the judged-demo chaos gate (05 §6.6) and the pre-demo security tests ST-15 (co-location) and ST-20 (demo security scenario) |
| **`P2·MVP`** | Required before production **and** in the G3 regression set; waivable for the demo only with a documented risk | The performance and capacity claims shown to the judges on the node ("measured, not asserted"), except those that bear on safety or a zero-tolerance invariant, which are `P1·MVP`: kill-switch propagation (PT-07), whole-fleet re-dispatch within one tick, opt-out and reserve-change enforcement latency, SBO timing and utility-override precedence, decision-trace and audit write overhead, M&V completeness, the AI agent never slowing control, and the memory ceiling — plus the operability evidence the demo relies on and the red-team exercise that ST-08 schedules before the demo (its flags are also proven by automated `P1·MVP` cases; a demo waiver needs a documented risk and a completion date before G4) |
| **`P2`** | Required behaviour that must pass before production (G4) | Production-shape HA, 100,000-hub evidence (E5), disaster-recovery drills and the migration rehearsal (E6), deeper variants of a decision already proven by a `P1·MVP` case, operability drills |
| **`P3`** | Depth beyond the MVP or post-production scope | Capacity-limit discovery beyond 100k, characterisation, yearly exercises, and the pre-connection gates for real hubs, real counterparties and real market access (security conditions SC-01…SC-19, §6.17) |

A **deeper variant** of a binding-decision case (another scope, another protocol, production shape) is `P2` when a
representative `P1·MVP` case already proves the decision on the node; §9.7 shows the representative case per decision.

| Automation | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `auto · per PR` | Runs unattended in CI (E2) on every pull request; verdict computed by oracles |
| `auto · nightly` / `auto · weekly` | Runs unattended in the nightly or weekly suite (E2), or in a scheduled node test window (E4) where the setup says so |
| `auto · per release` | Runs unattended for every release candidate (G2) |
| `auto · scheduled (…)` | Runs unattended on a calendar (monthly or quarterly drills, pre-demo and pre-cutover drills, §8.4) |
| `manual · scripted (…)` | Scripted and instrumented, but a human decides or observes a step (the strategy's `manual`) |
| `manual` | Human exercise with recorded evidence: tabletop, penetration test, red team |

The first word of the automation cell is the strategy's code (`auto` or `manual`); the qualifier after `·` is the cadence.

### 1.5 Environments

The environment codes are those of `01-test-strategy.md` §5.1; this table adds what the non-functional cases need from
each. Its safety rails (§5.2) apply unchanged and are made measurable in §1.8.

| Code | Strategy environment | Where, capacity and constraints | Used here for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `E1` | Developer laptop | k3d mirroring k3s; ≈ 500 simulated hubs | authoring and debugging load, fuzz and chaos scripts; **never evidence** |
| `E2` | CI | Ephemeral k3d on CI runners (4 vCPU / 16 GiB, 06 §7.1); 500–2,000 hubs; 10-minute performance smoke | per-PR gates, fuzzing, property tests, alert-rule unit tests, admission tests, supply-chain checks; nightly and weekly suites |
| `E3` | Node staging | 192.168.5.35, `node-demo` profile (2,000 hubs), continuous | read-only security checks between windows; the judged-demo security scenario (TC-SEC-148); demo rehearsals — **no destructive case runs on `E3`** |
| `E4` | Node test windows | The single k3s node 192.168.5.35 (Debian 13, KVM, 8 vCPU, 15 GiB) in a **scheduled, announced window** (06 ADR-508; strategy §11: 2026-10-07…17): allocatable **5,500 m CPU / 10,496 MiB for pods** (R2, 06 §1.3), CPU requests per the platform table ≈ 4.86 vCPU (R14), kubepods hard cap 11,008 Mi; profile `node-10k` (10,000 simulated hubs, `agent-sim` 2 × 5,000); host services (Apache, PHP simulators, Roundcube, Postfix/Dovecot/spamd/ClamAV, MariaDB, `fdmp`) are never touched; data reset after each window | PT-01…PT-07, PT-09, PT-10, PT-12, chaos, DR drills on the node, security tests against the real deployment |
| `E4-DR` | Node test windows (restore configuration) | `E4` plus the `og-scratch` class on `/` (≤ 40 GiB, 06 §1.4), `agent-sim` scaled down (a second PostgreSQL needs ≈ 2 GiB) | restore target of PITR and restore drills |
| `E5` | Performance cloud | Ephemeral managed multi-zone Kubernetes in the production cloud account with the node pools of 06 §2.2 (3 zones), CNPG 3 instances, NATS R3, EMQX cluster, Valkey + Sentinel; 100,000 synthetic hubs (20 `agent-sim` pods × 5,000, PT-08), up to 150,000 for limit discovery; budgeted per run | PT-08 and PT-11 (strategy §5.1 and G4; 06 calls PT-11's environment "prod-like"), 100,000-hub extrapolation, command fan-out and kill-switch propagation, zone loss, reconnect storms and broker or stream-leader loss at 100,000 hubs, limit discovery |
| `E6` | Production-like | Managed multi-zone cluster at reduced size (06 §2) before cutover, same signed images and bundles as production; after go-live retained as the pre-production environment (A) | production-shape HA at reduced load (hot-standby leaders, CNPG failover, Valkey Sentinel, Keycloak replicas), rolling and minor-version upgrades, region failover, KMS signing, the migration rehearsal; after go-live the yearly penetration test and red team (ST-01, ST-08) |

**Environment identities.** Every load generator, fault injector and simulator carries `env=test` identities and its own
NATS account (05 §2.6 test-harness isolation). On `E3` and `E4` the simulated hubs are real devices to the Orchestrator by
design (brief §1.2); on `E5` and `E6` they are enrolled under test-only roots that production brokers never trust (CTL-100).
After cutover the production fleet receives no test traffic except approved synthetic canary checks (strategy §5.1).

| `TOPO-DEMO` | 40 constrained banks plus zone pools, ≈ 500 hubs per partition average, 4 dispatcher partitions on `E4` (01 §14.2), 200 partitions on `E5` (01 §15) | 03 §6.8; 01 §11, §15 |

### 1.7 Measurement rules (apply to every case)

1. **Percentiles** are computed from raw per-event timestamps exported by the services and the load generator, not from
   Prometheus histogram interpolation (histograms feed dashboards only). A p99 needs ≥ 1,000 samples and a p99.9 ≥ 10,000;
   fewer samples is a **fail** ("insufficient evidence"), not a pass.
2. **Warm-up** (first 10 minutes, or until the twin covers ≥ 95% of hubs) and **cool-down** are excluded and reported.
3. **Clocks.** On `E3` and `E4`, `agent-sim` and services share the kernel clock, so hub-send → service-receipt latency is
   exact to the scheduler. On `E5` and `E6`, all pods are chrony-disciplined; a run is valid only if the measured cross-node
   offset stays ≤ 5 ms (A); sub-50-ms budgets are measured with in-process monotonic spans only. Latency, performance and
   certificate-validity measurements always use the wall clock, never the harness virtual clock (strategy §3.7); virtual time
   is used only where a case says so (for example multi-hour autonomy or day-35 deadlines).
4. **Generator validity.** Every performance or chaos run starts with `TC-PERF-067`; a run whose generator misses its cadence
   by > 1% or exceeds 70% of its own CPU limit is invalid and repeated.
5. **Evidence.** Each run stores, in the strategy's content-addressed evidence store (HC-TL-10), a Prometheus TSDB snapshot, the
   load-tool report, sampled Tempo traces, the Loki query export, the image digests, the Helm values hash, the dispatch-profile
   bundle digest and the fault-injection ground truth (HC-TL-07); performance evidence is archived to the backup bucket before
   the node's decommissioning (06 §9.2).
6. **Regression.** A result more than 10% worse than the last accepted baseline fails (06 §7.1 performance-smoke gate); per-service
   memory regression > 10% fails; a p95 MTTD or MTTR regression > 20% on an AP-H failure mode blocks a release (05 §6.5).
7. **Zero-tolerance invariants** are asserted in every run of every type by the strategy's invariant monitor (HC-TL-05;
   05 §6.5): 0 reserve or opt-out breaches; 0 double allocations (KPI-09); 0 rebounds above 95% of a bank rating (KPI-10,
   reviewer proposal — unverified, treated as hard); 0 executed commands to excluded, quarantined, islanded or opted-out hubs;
   0 accepted insecure SCADA sessions; 0 commands shown `CONFIRMED` without confirming telemetry; 0 invented M&V values; 0
   host-service impacts; and 0 synthetic personal-data canaries outside their allowed stores (PII canary scanner HC-TL-09).
   Any breach fails the run regardless of its own pass criteria and files an S1 defect (strategy §8.1).
8. **Reviewer-proposed numbers** (95% / 98% interval performance, ≤ 5-minute full output, ≤ 60 s northbound latency, ≥ 99%
   availability, 24-h M&V) are used as pass criteria only where they are the owning document's acceptance criterion, and are
   labelled "reviewer proposal — unverified" (R11).

### 1.8 Safety rails and global abort conditions for the shared node

Non-functional testing on 192.168.5.35 happens only in scheduled test windows, in namespaces labelled `og-chaos=allowed`, and
only against Orchestrator workloads (05 §6.1; strategy §5.2). Test windows never overlap node staging's demo rehearsals or
the judged demo. The following **global abort conditions (GA)** apply to every `E3` and `E4` case in
addition to each chaos case's own abort list. On any GA, the harness deletes all injections (`DELETE /v1/faults/{id}` on
agent-sim and grid-sim, Chaos Mesh experiments paused and deleted, toxiproxy reset), scales load generators to zero, records
the state and opens an incident.

| GA | Condition |
|---|---|
| GA-1 | Host-service impact: ALR-188 or ALR-502 firing, Apache `/opengrid/` p95 > 2× its pre-test baseline, or any signal from the host owner about mail, MariaDB or `fdmp` |
| GA-2 | Node memory available < 1 GiB (05 §6.1 rule 4). Driving `kubepods` to its 11,008 Mi cap is allowed only in the cases that test it (the cap, not the host, absorbs the pressure) |
| GA-3 | A zero-tolerance invariant breach (§1.7 rule 7) outside a case that deliberately provokes it in a fixture |
| GA-4 | `/var` free < 20 GiB (soft eviction threshold, 06 §1.2) or `/` free < 15 GiB |
| GA-5 | Any action that would touch host paths, host `/tmp`, MariaDB, the mail stack, Apache configuration beyond the reviewed include, or `fdmp` |
| GA-6 | Test or replay traffic (`env=test`) reaching a partition, settlement run or counterparty not labelled for test (FM-PLT-029) |
| GA-7 | More than one platform-level experiment active at the same time (05 §6.1 rule 5) |

### 1.9 Parameter set used by the pass criteria (binding decisions applied; conflicts flagged)

Where the source documents disagree, the value used here is the one the decision register makes binding, else the interim
value of `01-test-strategy.md` §13 (C-NN), else the stricter one; every conflict is listed again in §10 for a decision.
Proposed resolutions (R3, R4, R8, R9, R10) are tested as configured parameters whose default is the register's value
(strategy §0.2): if the user changes a default, fixture values change, not the test logic.

| Parameter | Value used here | Source | Conflicting value elsewhere |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pod memory budget | 10,496 MiB allocatable; kubepods cap 11,008 Mi | R2; 06 §1.3 | 05 §2.14 ≈ 11.5 GiB allocatable (to be aligned per R2) |
| Node CPU budget | Platform resource table authoritative: ≈ 4.86 vCPU requests of 5,500 m allocatable | R14; 06 §1.3 | 01 §14.2 ≈ 5.55 vCPU requests (to be trimmed per R14) |
| Control tick | 2 s during active firm events, 10 s otherwise | brief §4 | — |
| Command acknowledgement | p95 ≤ 2 × tick (4 s / 20 s) | NFR-014 | — |
| Command signer | `guardian` only | R1; 03-security §6.1 | 01 NFR-003 text still names `dispatcher`/`contracts` (update pending) |
| Command-signing algorithm | ES256 allow-list; EdDSA only after an ADR | 03-security §7.2; strategy C-07 | 02 §3.3 EdDSA primary, ES256 fallback |
| Leader election (R8) | NATS JetStream KV lease, TTL 6 s, renewal every 2 s, fail-static stop at 4 s, epoch = KV revision; epoch floors in KV `og-epochs`; failover ≤ 10 s with a hot standby | R8 (proposed); 01 §10; 06 §2.6, ADR-506 | 05 §2.2 TTL 10 s / renew 3 s (to be aligned) |
| Hub command lease | 30 s during firm events, 60 s otherwise; group heartbeat every 10 s | 05 RP-19 | 06 §3.5 `valid_until` ≤ 5 min (and ≥ 60 s in §2.6); 03-security FR-SEC-112 setpoint TTL ≤ 30 s; 03 §8.15 TTL = max(3 cycles, 30 s) |
| Local autonomy | After expiry, a valid signed fallback schedule runs only while the last orchestrator heartbeat is < 15 min old; otherwise safe mode (no export, no grid charging, reserve held) | register Q3 default; strategy C-06 | 02 §3.5 trigger after 5 min (event) / 30 min, export ≤ 15 min; 03-security §6.6 and FR-SEC-129 fallback only after 60 s without heartbeat and expiry |
| Confirmation tiers | Tier 1: ≥ 1 MW, or ≥ 25% of the target resource, or any bank-scope stop/block, or a change to declared capacity. Tier 2: ≥ 5 MW, any zone or fleet scope, fleet-wide mode changes, every kill-switch release, profile changes that alter priority or limits. Exception: a guardian-triggered or active-S1 risk-reducing stop executes on one confirmation; second approver co-signs within 15 min. Cumulative window 15 min per principal | R3 (proposed); strategy C-01, C-03 | 03 §8.15(b) 500 kW / 5 MW; 03-security §5.8 > 1 MW or > 1 bank / > 10 MW or > 1 zone; 07 §6.4 cumulative over 5 min and zone engage by one person; 05 RP-57 > 500 kW |
| Kill-switch engage and approval | Bank engage by `OP` (`FOP` as a parameter); approvers from a configured eligible set (default `APR` or `SEC`); invoker ≠ approver always | strategy C-02; register Q1 | FR-SAFE-006, FR-SAFE-008; 03-security A-15, A-17, SoD-03 |
| Kill-switch stop ramps | `SAFE_STOP` 30 s bank, 60 s zone, 120 s fleet; propagation ≤ one control interval; `ESTOP`/`EMERGENCY_STOP` is a separate no-ramp path only for the authorities 03-security permits | R4; NFR-019; strategy C-04 | 03-security §6.4 zone and fleet 60 s; 03 §8.16.1 stops without stagger |
| Kill-switch release staging | Tier 2; staged re-entry over ≥ 15 min (bank), ≥ 20 min (zone), ≥ 30 min (fleet) = max(A-DE-33 window, 15 min); aggregate step ≤ 10% of scope capacity per minute; rebound ≤ 95% of rating | R3, R4; strategy C-05 | 03 A-DE-33 10/20/30 min; 03-security §6.5 ≥ 15 min at every scope; 05 RP-58 bank ≥ 5 min |
| Stop path while `guardian` is down | **Open (R16).** Cases measure and report the stop gap; the verdict on it is blocked until R16 is decided | R16 | R1 makes `guardian` the only signer, so stops wait for it today (07, 03-security) |
| Emergency service-type pause | Allowed only as an emergency operational control: reason, Tier 2 approval, expiry ≤ 24 h, audited | R15 | 01 §16 feature flag pausing a service type (superseded) |
| Service certificate lifetime | 7 d, renewed at 2/3 (parameter; tests read the deployed value) | 05 RP-50 | 06 §8.4 30 d / renew at 20 d; 03-security §4.2 24 h |
| Device certificate lifetime | 90 d, renewed at 2/3, ±10 d issuance jitter | 05 RP-50 | 03-security §4.1 30 d |
| Command-key rotation | 60 min with 5-min overlap | 03-security §7.7 | 06 §8.3 operational keys ≤ 24 h |

---

## 2. Performance and scale (`TC-PERF`)

*Serves: Performance, Technical depth.* The node proves the 10,000-hub design point with real ingestion, control, guardian,
SCADA, API and database paths; `E5` proves the 100,000-hub model of 01 §15 and 06 §4 before any production claim. Every
case starts with the generator-validity check `TC-PERF-067` and asserts the zero-tolerance invariants of §1.7.

### 2.1 Telemetry ingestion and the digital twin

| ID | Title | Objective | Setup / environment | Load or attack profile | Steps | Pass criteria | Priority | Automation | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-PERF-001 | Steady telemetry ingestion, 10,000 hubs, 24 h | Prove the node ingests the design-rate telemetry without loss and keeps the twin inside its latency budget; this is the PT-01 core and the baseline for every other node case | `E4`, `node-10k`; agent-sim 2 × 5,000 hubs (mTLS to EMQX 8883); path EMQX → `device-gateway` → NATS `TELEMETRY` → `fleet-state` + ingest-writer → Timescale; `FW` off | `LP-S10`, 24 h after warm-up | 1) Run `TC-PERF-067`. 2) Start `LP-S10`. 3) At EMQX, NATS, twin and hypertable, audit sequence gaps per (`hub_id`, `boot_id`, `seq`). 4) Compute hub-send → twin-apply and hub-send → row-commit latency per message. 5) Sample twin freshness every minute | 1) Lost messages = 0 at all four audit points. 2) Telemetry → twin p99 ≤ 5 s (NFR-013) and p95 ≤ 2 s (A: 03 §3.4 design target). 3) Hub-send → row commit p99 ≤ 10 s (A). 4) Schema rejects < 0.01% (A: generator sends only valid payloads). 5) ≥ 99% of online hubs have twin data ≤ 30 s old in ≥ 99% of minutes (SLO-05 node target). 6) 0 restarts of `og-critical` pods; each within its memory request | P2·MVP | auto · nightly (10-min smoke per PR on `E2`) | Covers: NFR-013, NFR-510, PT-01, SLO-05, FM-COM-005 |
| TC-PERF-002 | Event burst: 10,000 hubs at 2-s cadence for 90 min | Prove the node sustains the worst planned event load — every hub reporting at 2 s while 20% get new setpoints each tick — without late firm ticks (PT-02) | `E4`, `node-10k`; all 4 dispatcher partitions in active firm events; `TOPO-DEMO` | `LP-E10`, 90 min | 1) Put every partition into a firm event from `REPLAY-ERCOT` fixtures. 2) Run `LP-E10`. 3) Record consumer lag per NATS consumer, tick phases per partition, command → ack latency and sequence gaps | 1) Consumer lag p99 ≤ 10 s (PT-02). 2) Tick duration p99 ≤ 1 s (PT-02). 3) 0 late firm ticks (tick completed > interval + 500 ms, SLO-01 definition). 4) Telemetry → twin p99 ≤ 5 s (NFR-013). 5) 0 lost commands and 0 lost acks. 6) Ack p95 ≤ 4 s (NFR-014). 7) Share of bus messages delayed > 5 s reported against NFR-211 (< 1%, §9.6) | P2·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: PT-02, NFR-510, NFR-013, NFR-014, NFR-515, SLO-01 |
| TC-PERF-003 | Realistic mixed day with fault weather, node SLOs | Prove the node meets its test SLOs over a realistic 24-h mix (30% of hubs in firm events) while background faults keep occurring | `E4`, `node-10k`; `REPLAY-ERCOT` day with firm windows, an `ERCOT_AS` award and energy arbitrage; all nine service types have active profiles | `LP-M10` + `FW`, 24 h | 1) Replay the day. 2) Compute SLO-01…05 SLIs from raw events. 3) Record per-interval delivered vs committed kW for every firm obligation | 1) SLO-01 ≥ 99.5% and SLO-02 ≥ 99.0% of ticks on time. 2) SLO-03 ≥ 97% of acks ≤ 2 × tick. 3) SLO-04 ≥ 90% of setpoints confirmed by telemetry ≤ 30 s. 4) SLO-05 ≥ 99% of minutes fresh (all node targets, 06 §3.1). 5) A per-interval performance ratio exists for 100% of firm intervals (NFR-007). 6) All §1.7 invariants hold | P2·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: SLO-01, SLO-02, SLO-03, SLO-04, SLO-05, NFR-007, NFR-519, NFR-520, NFR-521, KPI-01, R11 |
| TC-PERF-004 | Synchronized event start: 1,000 → 5,000 msg/s step | Prove the ingest path absorbs the step when every hub switches to event cadence within 10 s (worst-case simultaneous firm starts) | `E4`, `node-10k` | `LP-S10` for 30 min, then all hubs to 2 s within 10 s for 15 min, then back | 1) Trigger the step from grid-sim (all partitions start firm windows at the same interval boundary). 2) Measure publish-ack timeouts, consumer lag and twin latency per 10-s bucket | 1) JetStream publish-ack timeouts < 0.1% (A). 2) Consumer lag back below 5 s ≤ 120 s after the step (A). 3) Telemetry → twin p99 ≤ 10 s in the first 120 s and ≤ 5 s afterwards (NFR-013). 4) 0 lost messages. 5) Shedding ladder does not pass L1 (05 §2.8) | P2·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: NFR-013, NFR-510, PT-02, FM-COM-002 |
| TC-PERF-005 | Over-design spike degrades gracefully | Prove that at 2× the design rate telemetry is shed deliberately and accountably while commands, acks and firm control are protected (05 §2.8, CTL-062) | `E4`, `node-10k` | `LP-E10` with telemetry at 1 s (10,000 msg/s) for 10 min | 1) Start the spike. 2) Observe shedding-ladder entry (node CPU > 85% for 60 s, loop lag p99 > 200 ms, or tick > 80% of period). 3) Verify signed `telemetry_rate` down-sampling at L2. 4) Stop spike; observe exit | 1) 0 lost commands and 0 lost acks; ack p95 ≤ 4 s. 2) ≥ 99.5% of firm ticks on time. 3) 100% of dropped telemetry is accounted for by sequence-gap accounting of the down-sampling (FR-SEC-153). 4) EMQX memory ≤ 90% of its 1,024 Mi limit; 0 OOM. 5) Ladder exits ≤ 5 min after the triggers clear (05 §2.8). 6) ALR-041 fires if broker thresholds are crossed | P2 | auto · weekly | Covers: FM-COM-002, CTL-062, FR-SEC-153, TH-086, ALR-041 |
| TC-PERF-006 | Telemetry latency decomposition per segment | Localise latency so a budget breach points at one component, and prove every segment metric exists (measured, not asserted) | `E4`, `node-10k`; OTel spans on 1% of telemetry, 100% on sampled hubs | `LP-S10` 1 h, then `LP-E10` 1 h | 1) Tag 1,000 hubs for 100% tracing. 2) Collect segment timestamps: hub send, EMQX receive, gateway validated, NATS acked, twin applied, row committed | 1) p99 per segment (A): hub → EMQX ≤ 500 ms; EMQX → gateway ≤ 200 ms; validate + publish ≤ 50 ms; NATS → twin apply ≤ 1 s; twin → row commit ≤ 5 s (batches ≤ 250 ms, NFR-513). 2) Sum p99 ≤ 5 s (NFR-013). 3) `og_telemetry_e2e_latency_seconds` and per-segment metrics present on DASH-505 | P2 | auto · weekly | Covers: NFR-013, NFR-513, NFR-020, PT-01, DASH-505 |
| TC-PERF-007 | 100,000-hub telemetry: steady and event burst | Prove the production telemetry capacity and latency before any 100k claim (PT-08) | `E5`; 20 agent-sim pods × 5,000 hubs; KEDA scalers of 06 §2.4 active | `LP-S100` for 6 h (A), then `LP-E100` for 90 min | 1) Enrol 100k synthetic hubs under the test root. 2) Run steady, then burst. 3) Record lag, latency, scaling events and pod memory | 1) Telemetry → twin p99 ≤ 15 s (NFR-013, NFR-511). 2) 0 lost messages. 3) Consumer lag p99 ≤ 30 s (A). 4) `device-gateway` scales to ≥ 9 replicas ≤ 5 min after burst start (06 §2.4). 5) ≤ 40,000 connections per EMQX replicant. 6) 0 OOM kills | P2 | auto · scheduled (before go-live, then quarterly) | Covers: NFR-013, NFR-015, NFR-511, PT-08 |
| TC-PERF-008 | 100,000-hub peak mix of 01 §15 (≈ 53,000 msg/s broker total) | Prove the broker, bus and database hold the modelled peak including commands and acks, not only telemetry | `E5` as TC-PERF-007; 200 dispatcher partitions (`TOPO-DEMO` scaled) | `LP-P100` for 60 min | 1) Run the mix. 2) Record EMQX publish latency, NATS publish latency, ack latency by cadence and DB commit latency | 1) EMQX publish latency p99 ≤ 500 ms (05 FM-COM-002 threshold). 2) Ack p95 ≤ 4 s for event hubs, ≤ 20 s for normal hubs (NFR-014). 3) NATS publish p99 ≤ 250 ms (05 §2.3). 4) ≈ 12,000 telemetry rows/s committed with batch p99 ≤ 250 ms. 5) EMQX node CPU ≤ 70% (A: N-1 zone headroom) | P2 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-014, NFR-015, PT-08, FM-COM-002 |
| TC-PERF-009 | Scale-step validation of the capacity model and unit cost | Prove each 10× growth step is backed by measurement: resource per unit load within the model, cost per hub trending down | `E5`; steps of 10k, 25k, 50k, 100k hubs, 2 h each | `LP-M10`-shaped mix scaled to each step | 1) Run each step. 2) For EMQX, gateway, NATS, fleet-state, ingest-writer, PostgreSQL and dispatcher, compute resource per 1,000 msg/s and per 1,000 hubs. 3) Price the measured vCPU and storage with the 06 §2.8 unit prices | 1) Each component's measured unit cost is within ±20% of 06 §4 and 01 §15, or the model is re-baselined and signed off before the next step (NFR-015, NFR-514). 2) Cost at 100k ≤ $0.15 per hub-month (NFR-551). 3) $/hub decreases monotonically with scale (NFR-024) | P2 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-015, NFR-024, NFR-514, NFR-551 |
| TC-PERF-010 | Telemetry replay drain without starving live data | Prove the 24-h hub buffers can be replayed after an outage while live telemetry keeps its latency (head-of-line isolation) | `E4`, `node-10k` | `LP-S10` live + 3,000 hubs replaying 24 h each (8,640 msg per hub ≈ 25.9 M messages) at ≤ 20 msg/s per hub and ≤ 2,000 msg/s fleet | 1) Disconnect 3,000 hubs for 24 h on a virtual clock. 2) Reconnect them; replay on `TELEMETRY_REPLAY`. 3) Track live latency, replay backlog and M&V completion | 1) Live telemetry → twin p99 ≤ 5 s throughout. 2) `TELEMETRY_REPLAY` ≤ 5 GiB at all times. 3) Replay drained ≤ 4 h (A: 25.9 M ÷ 2,000/s = 3.6 h + 10%). 4) M&V backfill complete ≤ 6 h (06 §3.2). 5) 0 duplicate rows (idempotency keys, 05 §2.4) | P2 | auto · weekly | Covers: FM-COM-009, NFR-013, SLO-05, SLO-09 |

| TC-PERF-011 | Command fan-out latency at 10,000 hubs | Prove the command path (dispatcher → guardian → signer → NATS → gateway → EMQX → hub → ack → telemetry) meets its acknowledgement and execution budgets at the design burst | `E4`, `node-10k`; per-hub JWS ES256 | `LP-E10`, 60 min (1,000 commands/s) | 1) Run the burst. 2) Per command record publish, app-ack and `VERIFIED` (telemetry within tolerance) times. 3) Per tick record guardian, sign and publish phase times | 1) Ack p95 ≤ 4 s (NFR-014) and ≥ 97% of acks ≤ 2 × tick (SLO-03 node). 2) ≥ 95% `VERIFIED` within T_verify 6 s (05 §2.2) and ≥ 90% confirmed ≤ 30 s (SLO-04 node). 3) Guardian + sign + publish ≤ 500 ms p99 per tick (06 §4.7). 4) EMQX queue discards = 0 | P2·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: NFR-014, SLO-03, SLO-04, NFR-520, PT-02 |

| TC-PERF-013 | 100,000-hub command fan-out: per-hub JWS vs Merkle batch | Prove command throughput at production scale and produce the evidence for the batching ADR (03-security §7.3) | `E5`; variant A per-hub JWS, variant B Merkle-batched envelope | `LP-E100` commands (10,000/s + acks) for 90 min per variant | 1) Run A. 2) Run B. 3) Record signing CPU, message size, hub verification CPU and ack latency | 1) Ack p95 ≤ 4 s in both variants (NFR-014). 2) Variant A signing ≤ 0.5 core per 5,000-hub partition (A, 06 §4.7), or the result is reported as the trigger for B. 3) Variant B: one signature per partition per tick; message ≤ 1.2 KB (A). 4) agent-sim verification within its CPU budget in both variants | P3 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-014, NFR-015, CTL-042, PT-08 |

| TC-PERF-015 | Kill-switch propagation at 100,000 hubs | Prove the one-broadcast-per-scope design scales and measure the fleet stop as a grid event | `E5` | `LP-E100`; fleet-scope engage with 500 MW (A: 100k × 5 kW) of active discharge | 1) Engage fleet scope. 2) Measure broadcast delivery, stop ramp and aggregate fleet ramp per 60 s | 1) Delivery to reachable hubs p95 ≤ 2 s (NFR-019). 2) Stop ramp 120 s respected (R4). 3) Aggregate ramp reported per 60-s window; if it exceeds the 50 MW/min fleet envelope of G-04 the result is escalated for sign-off (§10 Q7) rather than silently passed | P2 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-019, FR-SEC-127, NFR-015, TH-014, D2, R4 |

| TC-PERF-023 | Time to full output after a firm event starts | Prove firm events reach contracted output inside the design target and the reviewer's bound | `E4`; 50 firm events from `REPLAY-ERCOT` representative days; contract sizes per profile; up-ramp = contract kW ÷ 3 per minute (R13) | `LP-M10` background | 1) Replay each event start. 2) Measure event start → sustained ≥ 100% of contract kW (15-s average) | 1) p99 ≤ 120 s (design, FR-DE-013). 2) 100% ≤ 300 s (NFR-012, reviewer proposal — unverified). 3) Start staggering window ≤ 30 s (FR-DE-089). 4) Signed `nbf` jitter present on 100% of start commands (G-06) | P2·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: NFR-012, KPI-03, FR-DE-013, FR-DE-089, R11, R13 |

## 3. Reliability and resilience (`TC-NFR-001…043`)

*Serves: Completeness, Technical depth, The problem.* The single-fault behaviour of every failure mode is already specified and
tested by its `TC-CHAOS` row in 05 (§4.1 below schedules them). The cases here add what those rows do not: the leader-election
and fencing contract of R8 end to end, dependency outages **under the design load** with measured recovery time, backlog drain
and data loss, the local-autonomy duration, and the production-only HA behaviours. Where a case shares an injection with a 05
row it says so and adds only quantitative criteria.

### 3.1 Leader election with epoch fencing (R8)

| ID | Title | Objective | Setup / environment | Load or attack profile | Steps | Pass criteria | Priority | Automation | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-NFR-001 | Dispatcher leader crash on the node | Prove the single-replica node deployment (`Recreate`, 05 §2.2) keeps the fleet safe and firm delivery intact while a partition has no leader, and that the restarted leader resumes bumplessly under a new epoch | `E4`, `node-10k`; firm event active on partition P1; lease store NATS KV | `LP-M10`; SIGKILL the P1 dispatcher 10 times at random tick phases, including mid-publish | 1) Kill. 2) Record partition mode, hub states (lease → fallback), epochs, restart and first command. 3) Compare first commands with measured output | 1) ALR-525 fires when leaderless > 2 ticks; AUTONOMOUS entered only after > 3 missed ticks in a firm event (05 §2.1). 2) Hubs keep the last setpoint until lease expiry (30 s), then the signed fallback schedule; 0 unplanned drops to 0 kW for a firm obligation (NFR-002). 3) ≥ 95% of affected 15-min intervals keep ≥ 95% of contract kW (reviewer proposal — unverified), and every shortfall is explained in the trace. 4) Restarted leader ready ≤ 120 s (05 §2.9) with an epoch strictly greater than before. 5) First command to each hub equals its measured output within tolerance (05 §2.12.7). 6) The observed behaviour matches the documented node HA posture (single replica, `Recreate`, leaderless handling per 05 §2.1), and the production-only properties are labelled deferred in the evidence (FR-OPS-010) | P1·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: FM-PLT-001, FM-DSP-030, NFR-002, NFR-527, ALR-525, ALR-160, R8, R11, FR-OPS-010 |
| TC-NFR-002 | Hot-standby leader failover in production shape | Prove the leader-plus-standby design fails over inside the 10-s budget without breaching a firm interval (06 §2.6) | `E6`; leader and standby per partition in different zones | `LP-E100` on a 5,000-hub partition; 20 SIGKILLs and 5 node drains of the leader | 1) Kill or drain. 2) Timestamp last command of the old epoch and first command of the new one. 3) Check hub fallback activations | 1) New leader issuing commands ≤ 10 s p95 and ≤ 15 s max after the kill (06 §2.6; 05 FM-COM-015). 2) Epoch equals the new KV revision, strictly increasing in 100% of failovers. 3) 0 fallback-schedule activations on hubs (failover shorter than the 30-s lease). 4) 0 firm-interval breaches caused (NFR-523) | P2 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-523, PT-11, FM-COM-015, FM-PLT-025, ALR-525, R8, FR-OPS-010 |

| TC-NFR-004 | Paused stale leader resumes | Prove the Kleppmann case — a leader frozen past its lease resumes and tries to send stale commands — cannot move a single hub | `E4`; `E6` repeat | `LP-E10`; SIGSTOP the leader for 15 s (> TTL), SIGCONT with commands still queued in memory | 1) Pause and resume. 2) Trace every command of the old epoch through guardian, `device-gateway` and hubs | 1) 100% of old-epoch commands refused: guardian does not sign, `device-gateway` rejects, hubs NACK `epoch` (DV-07). 2) ALR-526 (stale-epoch rejects > 0) and ALR-052 fire. 3) The stale process steps down ≤ 1 tick after resuming. 4) 0 hub executions of stale commands | P1·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: FM-PLT-025, FM-DEV-029, ALR-052, ALR-526, TH-161, R8 |
| TC-NFR-005 | Fencing enforced independently at every layer | Prove each enforcement point rejects stale or forged epochs even when the layers before it are bypassed (defence in depth) | `E2` harness with a test-only signer and direct injection points; `E4` confirmation run | Crafted commands: (a) epoch − 1; (b) current epoch with `seq` ≤ last applied; (c) an epoch above the current lease revision; (d) command (a) resubmitted while `guardian` and `device-gateway` restart | 1) Submit (a)–(c) to guardian. 2) Publish them past guardian to `device-gateway` (test signer, CI only). 3) Deliver them directly to agent-sim hubs. 4) Restart `guardian` and `device-gateway`; resubmit (d) during start-up and after readiness | 1) Guardian refuses to sign (a), (b) and (c) — it signs only the current lease revision. 2) `device-gateway` rejects (a) and (b). 3) Hubs reject (a) and (b) with reason codes. 4) `og_commands_total{result="stale_epoch"}` increments for each rejection. 5) After a restart both layers load the epoch floor from the NATS KV `og-epochs` before accepting any command (06 §2.6): (d) is rejected and 0 commands are accepted before the floor is loaded | P1·MVP | auto · per PR | Covers: FM-PLT-025, CTL-043, FR-SEC-117, NFR-031, D4, R8 |

| TC-NFR-007 | `scada-gateway` command-broker failover under load | Prove the SCADA command broker fails over with its replicated order and interlock state intact while events flow and SBOs are armed (complements TC-INT-716, which is functional) | `E4`; two adapter instances; state in NATS KV (07 §7.3) | 100 events/s; 20 kills of the active adapter, 10 of them between SELECT and OPERATE | 1) Kill. 2) Compare `COMMAND_SEQ`, SBO locks, latched restrictive states and event cursors before and after. 3) Count executions | 1) Standby takes over ≤ 10 s (07 §7.3). 2) Replicated state identical before and after in 100% of kills. 3) OPERATE while leaderless → `PROCESSING_LIMITED`; 0 double executions. 4) 0 Class 1 events lost; replay in order; integrity poll after reconnect | P2 | auto · nightly | Covers: FM-SCADA-013, FM-SCADA-035, TH-132, ALR-092, R8 |

### 3.3 Node loss, controlled resume and local autonomy

| ID | Title | Objective | Setup / environment | Load or attack profile | Steps | Pass criteria | Priority | Automation | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 4. Chaos engineering: execution of the existing catalogue and new compound scenarios

*Serves: Completeness ("including under injected failures"), Technical depth, Creativity.*

### 4.1 Execution index of the existing chaos catalogue (referenced, not restated)

rules in 05 §6.5; the game days are 05 §6.7. This document only binds them to environments, load profiles and gates.

`475`, `479`, `480`, `481`, `486`, `491`, `493`, `495`, `497`, `499` (SEC). **Production gate:** 100% AP-H, ≥ 90% AP-M,
≥ 60% AP-L automated; every row exercised at least once per quarter; nightly suite green three nights running before any
release. Each run publishes per-row MTTD and MTTR (05 §6.5) as CI artefacts (`TC-NFR-071`).

### 4.2 Rules for the new compound scenarios

Every compound scenario below goes **beyond** C1–C15: it crosses at least two failure categories that 05 treats separately.

1. **Steady-state hypothesis** — the objective states what must remain true; the run is judged against it and against the
   zero-tolerance invariants (§1.7 rule 7).
2. **Blast radius** — stated per scenario: the smallest scope (hub, bank, zone, fleet, service) the injections may affect;
   anything outside it that changes is a failure.
3. **Abort conditions** — the global GA conditions of §1.8 plus the scenario's own; an abort restores every injection within
   one control tick and is recorded.
4. **Tagging** — every injection carries an experiment ID; decision traces record `chaos=true`; chaos intervals are excluded
   from settlement and marked in M&V (05 §6.1 rule 6).
5. **One platform-level experiment at a time** on the node (GA-7); device and counterparty background faults may run
   concurrently.
6. **Evidence** — the trace, the alert timeline, per-injection MTTD/MTTR and the invariant report are stored per run.

### 4.3 New compound chaos scenarios (`TC-CHAOS-600…627`)

| TC-CHAOS-626 | Regional cellular outage during a 4CP partner event while the VTN is down | Steady state: a received partner event runs to its end and is measured honestly when both the hubs' links and the utility's VTN fail | `E4`; `PARTNER_CAPACITY` event received | **Injections:** area outage for 20% of enrolled hubs; OpenADR VTN down after the event starts. **Blast radius:** the program's hubs. **Abort:** GA | 1) Inject. 2) Track execution, measurement and reporting | 1) The received event runs to its end (FM-DSP-012). 2) Offline hubs follow leases → fallback; delivery measured at bank SCADA where available. 3) OpenADR reports stored and sent on recovery; P10 kW/hub computed only from reporting hubs and flagged (KPI-08). 4) Utility told of the telemetry gap. 5) ALR-042 and ALR-542 raised | P2 | auto · weekly | Covers: FM-COM-003, FM-DSP-012, KPI-08, ALR-042, ALR-542 |
| TC-CHAOS-627 | Zone loss plus a stream-leader loss in another zone at 100,000 hubs | Steady state: a double fault in production shape costs no firm interval and no committed data | `E5`; `LP-E100` subset | **Injections:** isolate zone A; 60 s later kill the NATS stream-leader node in zone B. **Blast radius:** two zones' workloads. **Abort:** firm-interval breach projected by the interval-rescue check | 1) Inject. 2) Track leaders, streams, database and API | 1) Majority continues; leader failover ≤ 10 s. 2) 0 firm-interval breaches caused (NFR-523). 3) RPO 0 for control data (NFR-524). 4) API recovered ≤ 60 s | P3 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-523, NFR-524, FM-COM-016, FM-COM-015, PT-11 |

## 5. Disaster recovery (`TC-DR`)

*Serves: Completeness, Usability, Technical depth.* Backups leave the node from day one (06 ADR-504), so the October migration
is a restore that has already been rehearsed. Every restore case ends with the audit-chain verification of `TC-DR-004`: a
restore that cannot prove its own integrity is a failed restore. Heartbeat writers (one row per second with a monotonic
counter, committed through the normal write path) make RPO measurements exact.

**Drill calendar tied to the migration plan (06 §9.1, assumed D = 2026-10-30):** PITR drill 1 on 2026-10-09 (node → scratch
namespace), drill 2 on 2026-10-16 (node backups → the production cluster, `E6`), full migration rehearsal 2026-10-19…21, go/no-go
2026-10-22, cutover 2026-10-26, node cleanup 2026-10-29.

| ID | Title | Objective | Setup / environment | Load or attack profile | Steps | Pass criteria | Priority | Automation | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-DR-001 | Point-in-time recovery drill on the node (PT-10) | Prove the database can be recovered to any chosen instant, fast and consistent | `E4-DR`; base backups + WAL in the off-node bucket (Barman Cloud plugin); restore into a scratch namespace on `og-scratch`; agent-sim scaled down | Source under `LP-M10` with a heartbeat writer; target instant T chosen at random within the last 24 h | 1) Record, at T, per-table row counts, settlement totals and the chain tip. 2) Restore to T. 3) Run `TC-DR-004`, `TC-DR-005`. 4) Empty the scratch volume | 1) PITR complete ≤ 60 min (PT-10). 2) Heartbeats present up to T and none after (± 1 s). 3) Relational row counts at T match ± 0. 4) `TC-DR-004` and `TC-DR-005` pass on the restored copy. 5) Scratch emptied; `/` free restored (NFR-501). 6) Weekly during the test phase; ≥ 2 passes before cutover (06 §3.4, NFR-526) | P2 | auto · scheduled (weekly) | Covers: PT-10, NFR-022, NFR-526, RB-522, RB-503 |
| TC-DR-002 | RPO measured on the node | Measure the real data-loss window after losing the database volume | `E4-DR`: a disposable restored cluster in the scratch namespace, archiving to the bucket (`archive_timeout` 60 s) | Heartbeat writer at 1 row/s | 1) Run 30 min. 2) Destroy the disposable cluster's volume without warning. 3) Restore from the bucket. 4) Count lost heartbeats | 1) Lost heartbeats ≤ 300 (RPO ≤ 5 min, NFR-524 node). 2) Median lost ≤ 120 (A: `archive_timeout` 60 s). 3) ALR-508 would have fired had archiving been failing (checked by `TC-DR-009`) | P2 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-524, FM-PLT-023, ALR-508, RB-504 |
| TC-DR-003 | Full node rebuild from Git and backups (RTO) | Prove the whole environment can be rebuilt on a clean host within the RTO | Spare VM or `E6`, with no prior state; Git tag, SOPS secrets, bucket | `make node-bootstrap` + `make deploy`, then restore and the resume sequence | 1) Rebuild the cluster and releases. 2) Restore PostgreSQL (local fast-restore copy variant and cloud-only variant) and NATS snapshots. 3) Run the resume sequence (06 §3.6) | 1) Cluster + releases (no data) ≤ 45 min (NFR-508). 2) Rebuild + restore → first valid dispatch ≤ 4 h (NFR-525, PT-10); ≤ 2 h with the local fast-restore copy (06 §3.2). 3) No host-specific value outside `envs/node/` needed (NFR-509) | P2 | manual · scripted (≥ once before cutover) | Covers: NFR-525, NFR-508, NFR-022, PT-10, RB-522 |
| TC-DR-004 | Audit-chain verification after a restore | Prove the tamper-evident ledger remains verifiable end to end after any restore, and that anything lost is counted, never silently absent | Restored copy from `TC-DR-001`, `TC-DR-003` or `TC-DR-011`; off-node anchors and time-stamp tokens | Verifier full run | 1) Run all seven verifier checks of 03-security §12.9. 2) Compare the restored chain tip with the latest off-node anchor. 3) Count records between the restore point and the newest anchor | 1) Sequence continuity, chain hashes, producer signatures (valid at signing time), checkpoint signatures and consistency proofs, anchors and RFC 3161 tokens all pass (FR-SEC-172). 2) Linkage completeness: every invoice line resolves to its call; every command has a verdict and a decision. 3) Sampled deterministic replay reproduces allocation hashes (FR-SEC-174). 4) Records after the restore point are reported as "lost since restore point" with their count; 0 undetected divergence | P2 | auto · scheduled (every restore) | Covers: NFR-028, NFR-529, CTL-073, CTL-143, FR-SEC-172, FR-SEC-174, CTL-138 |
| TC-DR-005 | Settlement and billing integrity after a restore | Prove money records survive a restore exactly | Restored copy | Daily recomputation job on the restored copy | 1) Compare sealed periods with the pre-incident export. 2) Recompute open periods. 3) Compare line hashes | 1) Sealed-period totals per customer and month identical (± 0). 2) Open periods recomputed deterministically with identical line hashes (FR-SEC-176). 3) DET-074 silent | P2 | auto · scheduled | Covers: CTL-142, FR-SEC-176, NFR-526, TH-154 |
| TC-DR-006 | NATS stream backup and restore | Prove the event, audit and command streams restore with their protections | `E4-DR` | Nightly `nats stream backup` of `EVENTS`, `AUDIT`, `COMMANDS` | 1) Restore into a scratch NATS. 2) Compare counts and sequences. 3) Try to purge `AUDIT` | 1) Message counts and last sequences match the snapshot. 2) Consumer positions reset deliberately and documented. 3) `AUDIT` keeps `deny_delete` / `deny_purge`; purge refused. 4) Restore ≤ 1 h (06 §3.2 node) | P2 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-525, NFR-526 |
| TC-DR-007 | Identity restore | Prove people can log in with the right roles after a rebuild | Restored PostgreSQL (Keycloak database) + realm-as-code from Git | Role-matrix smoke test | 1) Restore. 2) Apply the realm. 3) Log in as one user per role. 4) Scan exports for credential material | 1) Logins succeed and 100% of the role smoke matrix passes. 2) No credential material in any realm export. 3) Identity restored ≤ 4 h (06 §3.2 node) | P2 | manual · scripted | Covers: NFR-525, FM-PLT-016 |
| TC-DR-008 | Backup immutability and encryption | Prove a compromised writer or an administrator cannot destroy or read the backups | Bucket with Object Lock: governance for database backups, compliance for audit/billing anchors and Parquet exports | Delete and overwrite attempts with the backup-writer credential and with an administrator credential | 1) Attempt deletes and overwrites. 2) Inspect encryption headers. 3) Attempt a restore without the split-custody key | 1) Compliance-mode objects cannot be deleted or overwritten by anyone before expiry. 2) Governance-mode objects cannot be deleted without the explicit bypass permission, which the writer lacks. 3) 100% of objects encrypted (SSE-KMS). 4) Restore needs the split-custody key (CTL-097) | P2 | auto · scheduled (monthly) | Covers: NFR-526, CTL-097, TH-084, TH-091 |
| TC-DR-009 | Backup-failure detection and recovery | Prove a failing backup can never silently grow the RPO (complements TC-CHAOS-423) | `E4`; virtual clock for the 26-h and 35-day thresholds | Archive target blocked; base backups stopped | 1) Block. 2) Advance the virtual clock. 3) Execute RB-504. 4) Unblock | 1) ALR-508 ≤ 5 min. 2) ALR-509 once the last base backup is > 26 h old. 3) ALR-510 once no restore drill has passed for > 35 days. 4) RB-504 steps executed as written; archive catches up with 0 gap | P2 | auto · weekly | Covers: FM-PLT-023, ALR-508, ALR-509, ALR-510, RB-504 |
| TC-DR-010 | Logical backup safety net | Prove the weekly per-schema dumps restore with TimescaleDB structures intact | `E4-DR`; same TimescaleDB version | Weekly `pg_dump -Fc` per schema | 1) Restore with `timescaledb_pre_restore()` / `post_restore()`. 2) Inspect hypertables, policies and aggregates. 3) Run `TC-DR-004` | 1) Hypertables, compression and retention policies, continuous aggregates and jobs present and running. 2) `TC-DR-004` passes on the restored copy | P2 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-526, FM-PLT-008 |
| TC-DR-011 | Late-October migration rehearsal | Prove the node → production move works end to end before the real cutover (06 §9) | `E6` (the production cluster at `node-demo`-equivalent size, before cutover); bucket; rehearsal window 2026-10-19…21 | RB-525 steps §9.3 without the final switch of the real fleet | 1) Hold mode on the node; drain; final base backup. 2) PITR restore to the final LSN in production; restore NATS snapshots. 3) Re-point agent-sim through the DNS name; re-enrol under the production intermediate. 4) Resume sequence. 5) Run the §9.4 checklist. 6) Archive the performance evidence | 1) Relational row counts ± 0; settlement totals identical. 2) Audit chain verified across both segments and all anchors. 3) Every service type has an active signed profile with the pinned digest. 4) 100% of agent-sim hubs connected to production; 0 on the node's 8883. 5) Measured cutover duration within the planned window (A: ≤ 4 h). 6) Performance evidence (TSDB snapshots, reports) archived with checksums (06 §9.2). 7) Open issues have owners before the 2026-10-22 go/no-go (NFR-550) | P2 | manual · scripted | Covers: NFR-550, NFR-022, RB-525, TH-094 |
| TC-DR-012 | Cutover rollback path | Prove a failed cutover can be backed out safely | Rehearsal as `TC-DR-011` | Injected failure at step 3 (verification mismatch) | 1) Inject. 2) Execute the rollback: node authoritative again, Tier-2 release of the node's hold mode, agents re-pointed back | 1) Rollback complete ≤ 1 h (A). 2) 0 records diverge between the node and what production accepted. 3) Every step recorded in the audit chain | P2 | manual · scripted | Covers: NFR-550, RB-525 |
| TC-DR-013 | Audit chain continuity across environments | Prove the ledger spans the node and production without a seam an attacker could exploit | After `TC-DR-011` | Verifier over both segments; a tamper fixture on the junction record | 1) Verify both segments. 2) Modify one byte of the junction record in a copy. 3) Re-verify | 1) First production record's `prev_hash` equals the last node record's hash. 2) Full verification across both segments and both environments' anchors passes. 3) The tamper fixture is detected (DET-050) | P2 | auto · scheduled | Covers: NFR-529, CTL-073, FR-SEC-172, DET-050 |
| TC-DR-014 | Keys and secrets are regenerated, never migrated | Prove no demo trust anchor, key or credential crosses into production | After `TC-DR-011` | Cross-environment command and credential tests | 1) Send node-root-signed commands to production-enrolled hubs. 2) Compare secret fingerprints between environments. 3) After cleanup, try node credentials against providers and the bucket | 1) 100% of node-root commands rejected by production hubs (FR-SEC-116, NFR-545). 2) 0 identical secrets across environments. 3) Revoked node credentials fail everywhere. 4) SOPS age key rotated | P2 | manual · scripted | Covers: NFR-545, FR-SEC-116, TH-024, TH-094, CTL-100 |
| TC-DR-015 | Node cleanup verification | Prove the node is returned to its owner exactly as found (NFR-550, NFR-023) | 192.168.5.35 after cutover (D−1) | Read-only inspection plus the host owner's own checks | 1) Look for k3s processes, ports and files. 2) Check the Apache include and the nftables table. 3) Compare `/var` usage with the pre-install record. 4) Host owner checks `/opengrid/`, Roundcube, mail flow and MariaDB | 1) 0 k3s processes; ports 6443, 10250, 8883 and 30443 closed; `/var/lib/rancher`, `/var/lib/kubelet`, `/etc/rancher`, `/srv/og-scratch` (and `/srv/k3s` if used) absent. 2) Apache include removed and `apachectl configtest` OK; `inet og_k3s` table gone. 3) `/var` usage back to ≈ its pre-install level (≈ 10 GB used). 4) Host owner confirms all host services healthy | P2 | manual | Covers: NFR-550, NFR-023 |
| TC-DR-016 | Region failover in production | Prove the warm-standby region takes over within its RTO and fences the old region | `E6` with the DR region | Declared regional incident; heartbeat writer | 1) Promote the CNPG replica cluster. 2) Bump the region epoch. 3) Switch DNS (TTL 60 s). 4) Count heartbeats | 1) RTO ≤ 60 min; RPO ≤ 60 s (NFR-524, NFR-525). 2) Hubs reject old-region commands after the epoch bump (100%). 3) Twice a year | P2 | manual · scripted | Covers: NFR-524, NFR-525, RB-522 |

## 6. Security (`TC-SEC`)

*Serves: The problem, Technical depth, The "why".* The cases implement the security test requirements ST-01…ST-20 of
03-security §23 and prove the functional security requirements FR-SEC-101…203, the controls CTL-001…146 and the detections
DET-001…077 against the threats TH-001…161. Every attack runs only against Orchestrator workloads, with demo trust anchors and
simulated hubs (RR-01); nothing targets the co-resident host services, the real ERCOT account or any real counterparty.
Where a case needs attacker capabilities that production must never have (a test signer, a cloned key), those exist only in
`E2` or in `env=test` identities.

### 6.1 Device identity and the broker

| ID | Title | Objective | Setup / environment | Load or attack profile | Steps | Pass criteria | Priority | Automation | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-SEC-001 | Invalid device certificates are refused | Prove only valid hubs of the right environment can connect | `E2` (prod-configured trust store) and `E4` (EMQX 8883, LAN only) | 6 invalid classes × 100 attempts: expired, wrong CA, revoked, wrong EKU, SAN mismatch, self-signed; plus demo-root certificates against the prod-configured broker | 1) Attempt TLS + CONNECT per class. 2) Record TLS alerts and MQTT reason codes. 3) Connect a valid control certificate | 1) 100% of invalid attempts refused; 0 sessions (FR-SEC-101). 2) 100% of demo-root certificates refused by the prod-configured broker (FR-SEC-116). 3) Revoked-certificate attempts raise DET-010. 4) The valid control certificate connects | P1·MVP | auto · per PR | Covers: FR-SEC-101, FR-SEC-116, CTL-001, CTL-004, CTL-005, TH-027, TH-028 |
| TC-SEC-002 | Client identifier bound to the certificate | Prove a hub cannot present another hub's identity | `E4`, `E2` | 1,000 CONNECTs with hub A's certificate and hub B's client ID | 1) Attempt. 2) Record reason codes and logs | 1) 100% refused with reason 0x85 (FR-SEC-102). 2) 0 sessions for B's identity. 3) Every attempt logged with both identities | P1·MVP | auto · per PR | Covers: FR-SEC-102, CTL-007, TH-029 |
| TC-SEC-003 | Topic ACLs cannot be bypassed | Prove a hub can only publish to its own topics and read its own commands (the SUN:DOWN pattern, TH-029) | `E4`, `E2` | 10,000 attempts: publish to other hubs' telemetry and command topics; subscribe to others' command topics; wildcards (`hubs/+/cmd`, `#`); publish to signer-only topics | 1) Attempt from 20 authenticated hubs. 2) Count successes and detections | 1) 0 successful cross-hub publish or subscribe (FR-SEC-103). 2) DET-004 escalates a hub to QUARANTINED after ≥ 5 denials in 10 min | P1·MVP | auto · per PR | Covers: FR-SEC-103, CTL-008, TH-029, DET-004 |
| TC-SEC-004 | Duplicate identity (cloned certificate) | Prove a cloned hub identity is detected and isolated quickly | `E4` | A second device with hub H's certificate connects from another address; session take-over ping-pong | 1) Run 3 take-overs within 1 h. 2) Track detection and state | 1) Hub quarantined ≤ 10 s after the third take-over (FR-SEC-104, DET-003). 2) Both devices excluded from dispatch; IRP-01 opened. 3) M&V for the identity quarantined for the overlap (FM-DEV-015). 4) A fleet certificate audit finds no certificate shared by two hubs (FR-SEC-003) | P1·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: FR-SEC-104, DET-003, FM-DEV-015, TH-027, TH-040, IRP-01, FR-SEC-003 |
| TC-SEC-005 | Revocation takes effect within 60 s | Prove a revoked hub is cut off quickly even if CRL distribution is slow or down | `E4`; `LP-S10` | Revoke 20 hub certificates; variant with the CRL endpoint blocked (FM-SEC-014); variant: a hub returned under RMA and a home sold to a new owner | 1) Revoke. 2) Measure deny-list effect, session termination and CRL publication | 1) Reconnects refused ≤ 60 s after the decision (FR-SEC-106). 2) Active sessions of revoked hubs terminated ≤ 60 s (A). 3) CRL published ≤ 15 min. 4) With the CRL blocked, the deny-list still refuses 100%; ALR-231 raised. 5) RMA and ownership transfer: the old identity is revoked, the replacement enrols through the normal flow, and the previous owner's data and consents are not visible under the new owner (CTL-009). 6) `device-gateway` refuses commands to and telemetry from a revoked hub from the next control tick after the decision (FR-SEC-009) | P1·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: FR-SEC-106, CTL-005, DET-010, FM-SEC-014, TH-027, CTL-009, FR-SEC-009 |
| TC-SEC-006 | Attested enrolment and firmware allow-list | Prove fake or unapproved devices never receive dispatch | `E4`; step-ca with X5C / `device-attest-01` provisioners; `contracts` installation records | Enrolments (a) without an installation record, (b) without or with invalid attestation, (c) with simulated attestation, (d) with non-allow-listed firmware; (e) an enrolment spike > 3× expected installs/day | 1) Attempt each. 2) Inspect trust scores and commands received | 1) (a) and (b) fail 100%. 2) (c) enrols flagged with trust ≤ 60 (FR-SEC-105). 3) (d) receives only safe commands (G-13, DET-012). 4) (e) triggers DET-011 and holds enrolments | P2 | auto · nightly | Covers: FR-SEC-105, TH-028, TH-036, CTL-003, CTL-091, DET-011, DET-012 |
| TC-SEC-007 | Quarantine removes a hub from dispatch and M&V | Prove quarantine is immediate and complete | `E4`; firm event active | Quarantine 10 hubs mid-event | 1) Quarantine. 2) Inspect the next batches, the state estimator and M&V inputs. 3) Attempt a single-person release | 1) Next batch contains only safe commands for them (FR-SEC-107). 2) Their data leaves state estimation and M&V ≤ 10 s. 3) Release requires two people (A-20) | P2 | auto · nightly | Covers: FR-SEC-107, CTL-006, TH-041 |
| TC-SEC-008 | TLS handshake and connection flood on the broker | Prove a connection flood cannot lock out legitimate hubs or exhaust the broker (FM-SEC-008) | `E4` (listener LAN-only per decision register Q21) | 2,500 connections/s from a test host for 10 min (5× the listener limit) while 10,000 hubs are connected and 5% reconnect | 1) Flood. 2) Track bans, legitimate reconnect success and broker memory | 1) Source banned ≤ 1 min (DET-001: > 20 failures/min). 2) Legitimate reconnect success ≥ 99%. 3) EMQX memory ≤ 80% of its limit; overload protection sheds new connections before existing sessions. 4) ALR-227 raised | P2 | auto · weekly | Covers: FM-SEC-008, TH-032, DET-001, CTL-060, ALR-227 |
| TC-SEC-009 | Telemetry flood from compromised hubs | Prove one or ten flooding hubs cannot degrade the pipeline | `E4`; `LP-S10` | 1 hub, then 10 hubs publishing 100 msg/s for 10 min | 1) Flood. 2) Measure per-client limiting, detections and others' latency | 1) Per-client limit enforced (1 msg/s sustained, burst 10); excess discarded and counted. 2) DET-006 marks the hubs SUSPECT after 60 s. 3) Twin p99 for other hubs changes ≤ 5%. 4) ALR-220 P3 (P2 for ≥ 10 clients) | P2 | auto · weekly | Covers: FM-SEC-001, TH-034, DET-006, CTL-060, ALR-220 |
| TC-SEC-010 | Retained-message and Last-Will abuse | Prove a hub cannot plant stale or fake state through retained messages or a crafted Last Will | `E4`, `E2` | Hubs publish retained messages to key-set and group topics; Last Will payloads claiming "islanded" or "reserve 0%" | 1) Attempt. 2) Inspect broker state and the twin | 1) 0 retained messages accepted from hubs on restricted topics (only the signer may write them). 2) 0 twin state changes from Last-Will content; `OFFLINE` still requires the telemetry gap (RP-04) | P2 | auto · per PR | Covers: TH-035, CTL-008, FM-COM-019 |
| TC-SEC-011 | Every service link is mutually authenticated and encrypted | Prove NFR-017: no plaintext or unauthenticated internal hop exists | `E4`; attacker pod placed in an allowed namespace | For every link of 06 §1.7 (NATS, PgBouncer/PostgreSQL `verify-full`, Valkey TLS + ACL, OPA bundle fetch, OTLP, `dispatcher` ↔ `guardian` 8443): plaintext and certificate-less TLS attempts; in-cluster port scan | 1) Attempt each link. 2) Scan for plaintext listeners. 3) Map results to the container diagram arrows | 1) 100% of plaintext and certificate-less attempts refused (FR-SEC-155). 2) 0 plaintext listeners. 3) Every arrow of 01 §4 has a tested authentication mechanism (NFR-017), including the IEEE 2030.5 and OpenADR links to utilities, whose payloads cannot be altered in transit (TH-047) | P1·MVP | auto · per release | Covers: FR-SEC-155, NFR-017, CTL-010, CTL-066, TH-083, TH-047, FR-SEC-004, FR-SEC-010 |

### 6.2 Command signing (guardian as sole signer, R1), anti-replay and ordering (D4(a))

| ID | Title | Objective | Setup / environment | Load or attack profile | Steps | Pass criteria | Priority | Automation | Covers |

### 6.6 Anomaly detection and false-data injection

Each detection rule is code in Git with a positive and a negative fixture (FR-SEC-150). The six grouped cases below run every
fixture of their group; a rule passes when its positive fixture fires within the severity target of 03-security §17.2
(S1 in the SOC view ≤ 60 s, FR-SEC-149) and executes its automated response, and its negative fixture stays silent. Each
group also replays 24 h of clean `LP-M10` traffic: ≤ 1 false positive per rule per day (A).

| ID | Title | Objective | Setup / environment | Load or attack profile | Steps | Pass criteria | Priority | Automation | Covers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TC-SEC-060 | Detection fixtures — devices and broker | Prove the broker and device detections fire correctly and quietly | `E2` (fixtures) and `E4` (clean replay) | Positive and negative fixtures for DET-001…DET-012 (handshake failures, certificate failures, take-overs, ACL denials, connection rate, publish rate, source-network change, malformed payloads, flapping, revoked certificates, enrolment spikes, firmware allow-list) | 1) Run fixtures. 2) Measure time to detection and response. 3) Replay 24 h of clean traffic | 1) Every positive fixture fires within target and executes its response (ban, SUSPECT, QUARANTINED, staged admission, exclusion). 2) Every negative fixture silent. 3) ≤ 1 false positive per rule per day (A) | P2 | auto · per PR (fixtures), weekly (replay) | Covers: FR-SEC-150, CTL-054, DET-001, DET-002, DET-003, DET-004, DET-005, DET-006, DET-007, DET-008, DET-009, DET-010, DET-011, DET-012 |
| TC-SEC-061 | Detection fixtures — commands, guardian and physics | Prove the detections that guard the dispatch path and physical consistency | As TC-SEC-060 | Fixtures for DET-013…DET-030 (command without verdict, NACK rates, signing rate, key misuse, clip rate, measured ramp, mode-change share, bank loading, reverse flow, hub below reserve, frequency/voltage, step mismatch, AMS mismatch, energy balance, SCADA flatline, correlated anomalies, trust drop); out-of-band fixture: 5% and then 12% of one bank's hubs change mode with no signed command (a vendor-cloud path, TH-038) | As TC-SEC-060 | As TC-SEC-060; S1 rules (DET-013, DET-017, DET-019, DET-021, DET-022, DET-023) reach the SOC ≤ 60 s and trigger HOLD, LOCKDOWN or epoch bump as specified; the 12% out-of-band cohort raises DET-020 within one tick and the bank goes to HOLD (CTL-051), while the 5% cohort, below the rule's threshold, is reported as a residual detection gap (RR-03) | P1·MVP | auto · per PR, weekly | Covers: FR-SEC-150, CTL-056, DET-013, DET-014, DET-015, DET-016, DET-017, DET-018, DET-019, DET-020, DET-021, DET-022, DET-023, DET-024, DET-025, DET-026, DET-027, DET-028, DET-029, DET-030, TH-038, CTL-051, RR-03 |
| TC-SEC-062 | Detection fixtures — counterparties and people | Prove the detections for spoofed or anomalous counterparties and for identity abuse | As TC-SEC-060 | Fixtures for DET-031…DET-045 (counterparty authentication failures, nonconformant calls, call-rate surges, stacked kW behind a bank, idempotency-key reuse, origin change, cumulative sub-threshold actions, privileged login without WebAuthn, impossible travel, break-glass use, kill-switch engage/release, guardian change outside GitOps, bulk personal-data reads, out-of-process privileged grants, silenced alerts) | As TC-SEC-060 | As TC-SEC-060; valid-but-anomalous counterparty calls are executed and flagged, never refused (CTL-053) | P2 | auto · per PR, weekly | Covers: FR-SEC-150, CTL-053, DET-031, DET-032, DET-033, DET-034, DET-035, DET-036, DET-037, DET-038, DET-039, DET-040, DET-041, DET-042, DET-043, DET-044, DET-045 |
| TC-SEC-063 | Detection fixtures — platform, market and field | Prove the platform, integrity, market-conduct and field detections | As TC-SEC-060 | Fixtures for DET-046…DET-056 (unsigned image, exec into safety or data namespaces, egress off the allow-list, egress to node or LAN, chain verification failure, clock skew, KEV vulnerability, price sanity, TEEEF geofence, market conduct, opt-out surge) | As TC-SEC-060 | As TC-SEC-060; DET-050 freezes configuration changes and opens IRP-13; DET-054 issues a remote STOP | P2 | auto · per PR, weekly | Covers: FR-SEC-150, CTL-081, DET-046, DET-047, DET-048, DET-049, DET-050, DET-051, DET-052, DET-053, DET-054, DET-055, DET-056 |
| TC-SEC-064 | Detection fixtures — SCADA and `ai-agent` | Prove the protocol and AI detections | As TC-SEC-060 | Fixtures for DET-057…DET-069 (unexpected function codes, SA failures, unsolicited floods, time-set commands, operate without select, control-pattern anomalies, point-map hash drift, ICCP violations, denied agent tool calls, prompt-injection canaries, AI budget, personal data to the LLM, model-ID mismatch) | As TC-SEC-060 | As TC-SEC-060; DET-063 forces reduce-only SCADA controls; DET-066 terminates the session and quarantines the text; DET-068 blocks the request | P2 | auto · per PR, weekly | Covers: FR-SEC-150, CTL-115, DET-057, DET-058, DET-059, DET-060, DET-061, DET-062, DET-063, DET-064, DET-065, DET-066, DET-067, DET-068, DET-069 |
| TC-SEC-065 | Detection fixtures — configuration, audit and privacy | Prove the integrity and privacy detections | As TC-SEC-060 | Fixtures for DET-070…DET-077 (profile hash or unsigned load, version skew, personal data in outbound payloads, lineage gaps, invoice recomputation mismatch, deletion under legal hold, refusal codes outside the list, data-subject requests open > 35 days) | As TC-SEC-060 | As TC-SEC-060; DET-074 holds the invoice; DET-075 blocks the deletion | P2 | auto · per PR, weekly | Covers: FR-SEC-150, CTL-143, DET-070, DET-071, DET-072, DET-073, DET-074, DET-075, DET-076, DET-077 |
| TC-SEC-066 | False-data injection by hubs | Prove lying hubs are caught by physics and cannot fake delivery or hide an overload (AT-C C4) | `E4`; bank SCADA and simulated AMS data as independent sources | (a) 2% of a feeder's hubs over-report +5 kW phantom export, consistently; (b) 20% of a bank's hubs collude to hide an overload; (c) 50 hubs go silent at the start of each of 5 consecutive events and reappear afterwards (TH-114) | 1) Inject. 2) Track detections, trust, M&V inputs and control decisions. 3) Compare with grid-sim truth | 1) DET-025 (step mismatch > 10% on two steps), DET-026 (AMS mismatch > 10%), DET-027 and DET-029 fire. 2) Flagged data excluded from M&V (FR-SEC-145); physics check on every firm event (FR-SEC-146). 3) (b) control uses the more conservative value; 0 bank overload in grid-sim truth. 4) (c) their trust scores fall below 50 and they are excluded from firm allocation (FR-SEC-147, CTL-052); the pattern is reported to the operator | P1·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: TH-041, TH-042, FM-SEC-002, FR-SEC-145, FR-SEC-146, DET-025, DET-026, DET-029, CTL-051, TH-114, FR-SEC-147, CTL-052 |
| TC-SEC-067 | Spoofed, replayed, frozen, mis-scaled or utility-substituted bank measurements | Prove the closed loop never acts on a bank signal that is forged, wrong or manually substituted (AT-C C1–C3; R5) | `E4`; grid-sim RTU and an attacker master | (a) Unauthenticated injected measurement; (b) replayed measurements with old timestamps; (c) value frozen at "good" for > 5 intervals while peers move; (d) point re-scaled ×10; (e) a value the utility flags as substituted / manually forced; (f) the bank measurement toggled ±2% around the loop's threshold every 20–60 s, plus a stream of small setpoint changes each just above the deadband (TH-015, AB-15) | 1) Inject each. 2) Track classification, control mode and map checks | 1) (a) session refused (TLS + SA). 2) (b) classified stale. 3) (c) DET-028 → bad signal → hold, then schedule within one tick. 4) (d) plausibility and map checksum → DET-063 → reduce-only controls. 5) (e) displayed and usable for open-loop schedules only, never for closed-loop control; the quality flag is carried into the decision trace (R5, FM-SCADA-003). 6) 0 control decisions on bad data; ALR-081 / ALR-084 raised. 7) (f) no induced hunting: the loop is damped, then held with an operator notification (CTL-040); the bank's fleet output never reverses direction more than 3 times in any 60-s window (A) | P1·MVP | auto · nightly | Covers: TH-043, TH-044, TH-127, DET-028, DET-063, CTL-050, CTL-110, FM-SCADA-002, FM-SCADA-003, R5, TH-015, AB-15, CTL-040 |
| TC-SEC-068 | Detection-to-SOC latency | Prove security events reach analysts fast and cannot be deleted quietly | `E4` | 20 representative S1 and S2 fixtures | 1) Fire. 2) Time event → SOC view. 3) Check the security audit chain and both notification channels | 1) p95 ≤ 60 s to the SOC view (FR-SEC-149). 2) 100% written to the security audit chain (CTL-073). 3) Delivered over two independent channels | P2 | auto · nightly | Covers: FR-SEC-149, CTL-056, CTL-073 |
| TC-SEC-069 | Guard against mass auto-quarantine (security false positive) | Prove a misconfigured detector cannot quarantine a large share of healthy hubs and collapse firm capacity (FM-SEC-013) | `E4`; firm events active | Detector threshold fixture that flags 5% of the fleet within 10 min | 1) Deploy the fixture. 2) Track quarantines, alerts and firm capacity | 1) Auto-quarantine above 1% of the fleet in 10 min is held for human confirmation (A). 2) ALR-021 raised. 3) False quarantines cost ≤ 10% of any firm obligation's capacity (A); obligations notified per 05 §2.12.4 | P2 | auto · weekly | Covers: FM-SEC-013, ALR-021, TH-158, CTL-052 |
